(v,P_{t})=(v,P^{\prime}+P_{t}^{\prime\prime})=\left(v,P^{\prime}+t\sum a_{i}h_{ij}(v)Q_{j}+(1-t)P^{\prime\prime}\right).

Then
\rho(v,P_{t})=\rho(v,P)
for all
t
. Notice that
(v,P_{1})=\sigma(\rho(v,P))
. Therefore, every point in the fiber of
\rho(v,P)
can be connected with
\sigma(\rho(v,P))
. Hence fibers of
\rho
are all connected.
∎

3.5.
Proof of Proposition
3.16

Again, it suffices to consider the case that
V_{G}=W_{G}=\{0\}
.

Lemma 3.25
.

There exists a surjective submersion with connected fibers

\xi^{\prime}:M^{d}(G_{1}\times G_{2},V_{1}\oplus V_{2},W_{1}\oplus W_{2})\to M^{d}(G_{1},V_{1},W_{1})\times M^{d}(G_{2},V_{2},W_{2})

satisfying the following conditions.

(1)

\xi^{\prime}\circ\xi
is the identity map.

(2)

For each
(v,P)
in the domain of
\xi^{\prime}
,
{\rm ev}(\xi^{\prime}(v,P))={\rm ev}(v,P)
.

Proof.

For each
P\in{\rm Poly}_{G_{1}\times G_{2}}^{d}(V_{1}\oplus V_{2},W_{1}\oplus W_{2})
, denote its
W_{1}
-component by
P_{1}
and its
W_{2}
-component by
P_{2}
. Then we can regard
P_{1}
as a
G_{2}
-invariant map

P_{1}\in{\rm Poly}_{G_{2}}^{d}(V_{2},{\rm Poly}_{G_{1}}^{d}(V_{1},W_{1}))

and regard
P_{2}
as a
G_{1}
-invariant polynomial map

P_{2}\in{\rm Poly}_{G_{1}}^{d}(V_{1},{\rm Poly}_{G_{2}}^{d}(V_{2},W_{2})).

Then for
v=(v_{1},v_{2})\in V_{1}\oplus V_{2}
, define

\xi^{\prime}(v,P)=\Big((v_{1},P_{1}(\cdot,v_{2})),(v_{2},P_{2}(v_{1},\cdot))\Big).

Then it is easy to verify that
\xi^{\prime}\circ\xi
is the identity map and that

{\rm ev}(\xi^{\prime}(v,P))={\rm ev}(v,P).

Further, it is obvious that
\xi^{\prime}
is a surjective submersion. Moreover, if
\xi^{\prime}(v,P)=\xi^{\prime}(u,Q)
, then
v=u
and
\xi^{\prime}(v,P)=\xi^{\prime}(v,tP+(1-t)Q)
for all
t\in[0,1]
. Hence
\xi^{\prime}
has connected fibers.
∎

Proof of Proposition
3.16
.

Notice that the analytic submersion
\xi^{\prime}
preserves the action stratification. Then by Proposition
B.20
,
\xi^{\prime}|_{U^{d}}
pulls back the canonical Whitney stratification on
Z_{1}^{d}\times Z_{2}^{d}
to the canonical one on
Z^{d}
. The former is the product
{\mathfrak{Z}}_{1}^{d}\times{\mathfrak{Z}}_{2}^{d}
by Proposition
3.8
. Therefore

(\xi^{\prime})^{*}({\mathfrak{Z}}_{1}^{d}\times{\mathfrak{Z}}_{2}^{d})={\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}.

Then as
\xi^{\prime}\circ\xi
is the identity,
\xi
is transverse to
(\xi^{\prime})^{*}({\mathfrak{Z}}_{1}^{d}\times{\mathfrak{Z}}_{2}^{d})
. Hence

\xi^{*}{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}=\xi^{*}{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}=\xi^{*}((\xi^{\prime})^{*}({\mathfrak{Z}}_{1}^{d}\times{\mathfrak{Z}}_{2}^{d}))=(\xi^{\prime}\circ\xi)^{*}({\mathfrak{Z}}_{1}^{d}\times{\mathfrak{Z}}_{2}^{d})={\mathfrak{Z}}_{1}^{d}\times{\mathfrak{Z}}_{2}^{d}.\qed

3.6.
Proof of Proposition
3.17

Again, it suffices to consider the case when
V_{G}=W_{G}=R_{G}=0
; in particular,
{\rm proj}_{\check{R}_{G}}
is the identity map of
R
. Consider equivariant polynomial maps from
V\oplus R
to
W\oplus R
. A polynomial map from
V\oplus R
to
W\oplus R
consists of a polynomial map from
V\oplus R
to
W
and a polynomial map from
V\oplus R
to
R
. For convenience we write them as

\displaystyle\ P\in{\rm Poly}_{G}^{d}(V\oplus R,W),\

\displaystyle\ {\rm Id}_{R}+Q\in{\rm Poly}_{G}^{d}(V\oplus R,R).

Denote variables of
V\oplus R
be
(x,y)
and the image of the map
\eta
in (
3.4
) by

\widetilde{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(V\oplus R,W\oplus R)\subseteq{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(V\oplus R,W\oplus R),

i.e., polynomial maps
(P_{0},{\rm Id}_{R})
where
P_{0}:V\to W
does not depends on
y\in R
. We introduce an intermediate space

\widecheck{\rm Poly}_{G}(V\oplus R,W\oplus R)

consisting of pairs of the form

(x,y)\mapsto(P_{0}(x),y+Q_{0}(x)),\ {\rm where}\ P_{0}\in{\rm Poly}_{G}(V,W),\ Q_{0}\in{\rm Poly}_{G}(V,R).

Then consider

\widecheck{Z}^{d}(G,V\oplus R,W\oplus R)=Z^{d}(G,V\oplus R,W\oplus R)\cap\Big((V\oplus R)\times\widecheck{\rm Poly}_{G}(V\oplus R,W\oplus R)\Big)

By Proposition
3.6
, there are a canonical Whitney stratification
\widecheck{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(G,V\oplus R,W\oplus R)
on
\widecheck{Z}^{d}(G,V\oplus R,W\oplus R)
and a canonical Whitney stratification
\widetilde{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(G,V\oplus R,W\oplus R)
on
\widetilde{Z}^{d}(G,V\oplus R,W\oplus R)
. The latter can be identified with
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(G,V,W)
. Then we can factorize the map
\eta
as

\eta=\eta_{1}\circ\eta_{2}

where

\eta_{1}:(V\oplus R)\times\widecheck{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(V\oplus R,W\oplus R)\to(V\oplus R)\times{\rm Poly}_{G}^{d}(V\oplus R,W\oplus R)

and

\eta_{2}:(V\oplus R)\times\widetilde{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(V\oplus R,W\oplus R)\to(V\oplus R)\times\widecheck{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(V\oplus R,W\oplus R)

are both the natural inclusions.

Lemma 3.26
.

There exists
d_{3}
(depending on
G
,
V
,
W
, and
R
) such that when
d\geq d_{2}
,
\eta_{2}
is transverse to
\widecheck{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(G,V\oplus R,W\oplus R)
and

\eta_{2}^{*}\widecheck{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(G,V\oplus R,W\oplus R)=\widetilde{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(G,V\oplus R,W\oplus R).

Lemma 3.27
.

There exists
d_{4}
(depending on
G
,
V
,
W
, and
R
) such that when
d\geq d_{3}
,
\eta_{1}
is transverse to
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(G,V\oplus R,W\oplus R)
and

\eta_{1}^{*}{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(G,V\oplus R,W\oplus R)\equiv\widecheck{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(G,V\oplus R,W\oplus R).

Assuming these two lemmas, we can prove Proposition
3.17
.

Proof of Proposition
3.17
.

Let
d
be no smaller than
d_{1}
of Proposition
3.14
,
d_{3}
of Lemma
3.26
, and
d_{4}
of Lemma
3.27
. Lemma
3.26
and Lemma
3.27
imply that
\eta
is transverse to
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}={\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(G,V\oplus R,W\oplus R)
and

\eta^{*}{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}=(\eta_{1}\circ\eta_{2})^{*}{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}=\eta_{2}^{*}\eta_{1}^{*}{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}\equiv\eta_{2}^{*}\widecheck{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}\equiv\widetilde{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}.

If
l\geq d_{1}
and
d\geq l
, then by Proposition
3.14
, the composition
\eta\circ\sigma_{dl}
is also transverse to
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}
and

(\eta\circ\sigma_{dl})^{*}{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}\equiv\sigma_{dl}^{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}=\widetilde{\mathfrak{Z}}^{l}.

Therefore the transversality claim about
\eta
is true for
d\geq d_{1}(G,V,W)
. As
\widetilde{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}
has connected strata, it is equal to the refined pullback
\eta^{\dagger}{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}
. Moreover, when
d^{\prime}>d
, the arrows of the diagram (
3.5
) are all induced from refined pullbacks of Whitney stratifications. As refined pullback is functorial, the diagram commutes.
∎

The proof strategy of Lemma
3.26
and Lemma
3.27
is similar to those of Proposition
3.14
and Proposition
3.16
. We need to construct a submersion in the opposite direction.

Proof of Lemma
3.26
.

Define

\begin{split}\zeta_{2}:(V\oplus R)\times\widecheck{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(V\oplus R,W\oplus R)&\to(V\oplus R)\times\widetilde{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(V\oplus R,W\oplus R)\\
(x,y,P_{0},{\rm Id}_{R}+Q_{0})&\mapsto(x,y+Q_{0}(x),P_{0},{\rm Id}_{R}).\end{split}

It is straightforward to check that
\zeta_{2}
is a surjective submersion, preserves the action stratification, and
\zeta_{2}^{-1}(\widetilde{Z}^{d})=\widecheck{Z}^{d}
. Hence by Proposition
B.20
, one has
\zeta_{2}^{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}\equiv\widecheck{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}
. Moreover, it is easy to see that
\zeta_{2}
has connected fibers. So
\zeta_{2}^{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}=\widecheck{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}
. Moreover, since
\zeta_{2}\circ\eta_{2}
is the identity, one has

\eta_{2}^{*}\widecheck{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}=\eta_{2}^{*}(\zeta_{2}^{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d})=(\zeta_{2}\circ\eta_{2})^{*}\widetilde{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}=\widetilde{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}.\qed

Now we prove Lemma
3.27
. The construction of a left inverse of
\eta_{1}
is more involved. First we need the following result.

Proposition 3.28
.

[
Sch80
, Proposition 6.8]
Let
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
be finite-dimensional representations of
G
and let
U_{1}\subseteq V_{1}
be a
G
-invariant open subset. Then the space of
G
-invariant holomorphic maps
{\rm Map}_{G}^{h}(U_{1},V_{2})
from
U_{1}
to
V_{2}
is a module over the space of
G
-invariant holomorphic functions
{\rm Map}_{G}^{h}(U_{1},{\mathbb{C}})
generated by
{\rm Poly}_{G}(V_{1},V_{2})
.

Proof of Lemma
3.27
.

Define a kind of “left inverse” to
\eta_{1}
. For any
d\geq 0
and any
Q\in{\rm Poly}_{G}^{d}(V\oplus R,R)
, consider the equation

y+Q(x,y)=0.

By the implicit function theorem, there exists a
G
-invariant open neighborhood

O^{d}\subseteq V\times{\rm Poly}_{G}^{d}(V\oplus R,R)

of
V\times{\rm Poly}_{G}^{d}(V,R)
, where the second factor is viewed as elements in
{\rm Poly}_{G}^{d}(V\oplus R,R)
not depending on
R
, such that when
(x,Q)\in O^{d}
, we can always solve
y
in terms of
x
. More precisely, there is a
G
-equivariant complex analytic map

{\mathcal{F}}:O^{d}\to R

such that

{\mathcal{F}}(x,Q)+Q(x,{\mathcal{F}}(x,Q))\equiv 0\ {\rm and}\ {\mathcal{F}}(x,Q_{0})=-Q_{0}(x),\ \forall Q_{0}\in{\rm Poly}_{G}^{d}(V,R).

Notice that
{\mathcal{F}}:O^{d}\to R
is no longer a polynomial map. However, by Proposition
3.28
, we can write it as a combination

{\mathcal{F}}(x,Q)=\sum_{j=1}^{n}f_{j}(x,Q)P_{j}(x,Q)

where
f_{j}:O^{d}\to{\mathbb{C}}
is a
G
-invariant holomorphic function and
P_{j}
is
G
-equivariant polynomial map from
V\oplus{\rm Poly}_{G}^{d}(V\oplus R,R)
to
R
.

Now set

U^{d}:=\Big\{(x,y,P,{\rm Id}_{R}+Q)\in(V\oplus R)\times{\rm Poly}_{G}^{d}(V\oplus R,W\oplus R)\ |\ {\rm Id}_{R}+Q\in O^{d}\Big\}

which is a
G
-invariant open subset of
(V\oplus R)\times{\rm Poly}_{G}^{d}(V\oplus R,W\oplus R)
. Define

\begin{split}\widecheck{\zeta}_{1}:U^{d}&\to(V\oplus R)\times\widecheck{\rm Poly}{}_{G}(V\oplus R,W\oplus R)\\
(x,y,P,{\rm Id}_{R}+Q)&\ \mapsto\left(x,y,\widecheck{P},{\rm Id}_{R}+\widecheck{Q}\right).\end{split}

where

\displaystyle\ \widecheck{P}(\cdot)=P\left(\cdot,\sum_{j=1}^{n}f_{j}(x,Q)P_{j}(\cdot,Q)\right),\

\displaystyle\ \widecheck{Q}(\cdot)=Q\left(\cdot,\sum_{j=1}^{n}f_{j}(x,Q)P_{j}(\cdot,Q)\right).

Indeed,
\widecheck{P}
and
\widecheck{Q}
are polynomial maps in variable
x\in V
because
P
,
Q
are polynomials in
x,y
and
P_{j}
are polynomials in
x
. As
f_{j}
is
G
-invariant and
P_{j}
is
G
-equivariant, one can also check
\widecheck{P}
and
\widecheck{Q}
are equivariant. Moreover, there exists
\widecheck{d}\geq d
(depending on
d
,
G
,
V
,
W
) such that
\widecheck{P}
and
\widecheck{Q}
have degrees at most
\widecheck{d}
. Hence we obtain an analytic map

\widecheck{\zeta}_{1}:U^{d}\to(V\oplus R)\times\widecheck{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{\widecheck{d}}(V\oplus R,W\oplus R).

Claim.
Let

\widecheck{\phi}:(V\oplus R)\times\widecheck{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(V\oplus R,W\oplus R)\to(V\oplus R)\times\widecheck{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{\widecheck{d}}(V\oplus R,W\oplus R)

be the natural inclusion. Then
\widecheck{\zeta}_{1}\circ\eta_{1}=\widecheck{\phi}
.

Proof of the claim.
Indeed, if we insert
P=P_{0}
and
Q=Q_{0}
into the above formula of
\widecheck{P}
and
\widecheck{Q}
, as
P_{0}
and
Q_{0}
only depend on
x
, it follows that
\widecheck{P}=P_{0}
and
\widecheck{Q}=Q_{0}
.
End of the proof of the claim.

On the other hand, similar to the proof of Proposition
3.14
, there exists a
G
-invariant left inverse obtained by expressing high degree monomials in terms of low degree ones

\widecheck{\psi}:(V\oplus R)\times\widecheck{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{\widecheck{d}}(V\oplus R,W\oplus R)\to(V\oplus R)\times\widecheck{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(V\oplus R,W\oplus R)

to
\widecheck{\phi}
. Consider the composition

\zeta_{1}:=\widecheck{\psi}\circ\widecheck{\zeta}_{1}:U^{d}\to(V\oplus R)\times\widecheck{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(V\oplus R,W\oplus R)

Now we check the following items.

(1)

\zeta_{1}
preserves the action stratification.

(2)

By the above claim,
\zeta_{1}
is a left inverse to
\eta_{1}
.

(3)

\zeta_{1}^{-1}(\widecheck{Z}^{d})=Z^{d}\cap U^{d}
.
8
8

8

Notice that
\zeta_{1}
does not preserve the evaluation but only preserves the zero set.
Indeed, if
\widecheck{P}(x)=0
and
y+\widecheck{Q}(x)=0
, then

P(x,{\mathcal{F}}(x,Q))=0,\ y+Q(x,{\mathcal{F}}(x,Q))=0,

then by the definition of
{\mathcal{F}}(x,Q)
, it follows that
y={\mathcal{F}}(x,Q)
. Hence
P(x,y)=0
and
y+Q(x,y)=0
. Hence
\zeta_{1}^{-1}(\widecheck{Z}^{d})\subseteq Z^{d}
. On the other hand, if
(x,y,P,{\rm Id}_{R}+Q)\in Z^{d}\cap U^{d}
, then
P(x,y)=0
and
y+Q(x,y)=0
and
y={\mathcal{F}}(x,Q)
is the only solution. Hence
\zeta_{1}(Z^{d}\cap U^{d})=\widecheck{Z}^{d}
.

Then by Proposition
B.20
, one has

\zeta_{1}^{*}\widecheck{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}\equiv{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}|_{Z^{d}\cap U^{d}}.

On the other hand, as
\zeta_{1}\circ\eta_{1}
is the identity map,
\eta_{1}
is transverse to
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}
. Hence

\eta_{1}^{*}{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}=\eta_{1}^{*}({\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}|_{Z^{d}\cap U^{d}})\equiv\eta_{1}^{*}(\zeta_{1}^{*}\widecheck{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d})=(\zeta_{1}\circ\eta_{1})^{*}\widecheck{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}=\widecheck{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}.\qed

3.7.
Proof of Proposition
3.15

From the previous proofs of various properties of the canonical Whitney stratification, one can see the pattern is to consider the inclusion map of a space of special polynomial maps to a larger space of more general polynomial maps and then create a left inverse, which is typically an analytic submersion. In those situations, the comparisons of Whitney stratifications appeal to Proposition
B.20
. However, the construction for proving Proposition
3.15
here is more complicated. The map we constructed is no long an analytic submersion nor a left inverse. As a result, one needs to appeal to Lemma
B.6
which is more technical than Proposition
B.20
.

Definition 3.29
.

An
averaging map
is an
H
-equivariant smooth map of the form

\kappa:M^{d}(H,V,W)_{H}^{+}\to M^{d}(G,V,W)_{H}^{+},\ \kappa(v,Q)=(v,\underline{\smash{\kappa}}(v,Q)).

such that
{\rm ev}(\kappa(v,Q))=Q(v)\in W
.

Roughly speaking, an averaging map provides a way of producing a (more symmetric)
G
-equivariant map from a (less symmetric)
H
-equivariant map which preserves the evaluation map.

The proof of Proposition
3.15
is based on the following three lemmas.

Lemma 3.30
.

When
d
is sufficiently large, there exists an averaging map.

Proof.

See Subsubection
3.7.1
.
∎

The next two lemmas are to verify part of the assumptions of Lemma
B.6
.

Lemma 3.31
.

For any averaging map
\kappa
, the following are true.

(1)

\kappa\circ\mu:M^{d}(G,V,W)_{H}^{+}\to M^{d}(G,V,W)_{H}^{+}
is transverse to
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(G,V,W)

(\kappa\circ\mu)^{*}{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(G,V,W)={\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(G,V,W)|_{Z^{d}(G,V,W)_{H}^{+}}.

(2)

\mu\circ\kappa:M^{d}(H,V,W)_{H}^{+}\to M^{d}(H,V,W)_{H}^{+}
is transverse to
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(H,V,W)
and

(\mu\circ\kappa)^{*}{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(H,V,W)={\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(H,V,W)|_{Z^{d}(H,V,W)_{H}^{+}}.

Proof.

See Section
3.7.3
.
∎

Lemma 3.32
.

Suppose there exists an averaging map
\kappa
. Then
\mu|_{M^{d}(G,V,W)_{H}^{+}}
is transverse to
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(H,V,W)
and
\kappa
is transverse to
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(G,V,W)|_{Z^{d}(G,V,W)_{H}^{+}}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.31
(cf. Lemma
B.4
),
\mu
is transverse to
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(H,V,W)
at points on
{\rm Im}(\kappa)
where
\kappa
is any averaging map. To show that
\mu
is transverse to
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(H,V,W)
, it suffices to show that any
(v_{0},P_{0})\in Z^{d}(G,V,W)_{H}^{+}
lies in the image of some averaging map. Let
\kappa
be an averaging map. Consider the following vector field on
M^{d}(G,V,W)_{H}^{+}

w_{P_{0}}(v,P)=\Big(0,P_{0}-\underline{\smash{\kappa}}(\mu(v,P_{0}))\Big).

Its flow is the family of diffeomorphisms

F_{P_{0},t}(v,P)=\Big(v,P+t(P_{0}-\underline{\smash{\kappa}}(\mu(v,P_{0})))\Big).

It is easy to see that
F_{P_{0},t}
preserves the evaluation map and is
H
-equivariant. Hence the composition

F_{P_{0},t}\circ\kappa:M^{d}(H,V,W)_{H}^{+}\to M^{d}(G,V,W)_{H}^{+}

is an averaging map. Moreover,

F_{P_{0},1}\circ\kappa(\mu(v_{0},P_{0}))=\Big(v_{0},\underline{\smash{\kappa}}(\mu(v_{0},P_{0}))+(P_{0}-\underline{\smash{\kappa}}(\mu(v_{0},P_{0})))\Big)=(v,P_{0}).

Hence
(v_{0},P_{0})
is on the image of some averaging map. Therefore,
\mu
is transverse to
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(H,V,W)
.

The case for
\kappa
is similar. Lemma
3.31
(cf. Lemma
B.4
) implies that
\kappa
is transverse to
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(G,V,W)
at points on
{\rm Im}(\mu)
. We would like to show that
\kappa
is transverse to
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(G,V,W)
at any given
(v_{0},Q_{0})\in Z^{d}(H,V,W)_{H}^{+}
. Define the diffeomorphism

g_{Q_{0}}(v,Q)=\Big(v,Q+Q_{0}-\underline{\smash{\mu}}(\kappa(v,Q_{0}))\Big).

Then
g_{Q_{0}}
preserves the evaluation map. Then the composition
\kappa\circ g_{Q_{0}}
is also an averaging map and hence
\kappa\circ g_{Q_{0}}
is transverse at points on
{\rm Im}(\mu)
. In particular, it is transverse at the point
\mu(\kappa(v_{0},Q_{0}))
. But this is equivalent to that
\kappa
is transverse to
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(G,V,W)
at the point

g_{Q_{0}}(\mu(\kappa(v_{0},Q_{0})))=\Big(v_{0},\underline{\smash{\mu}}(\kappa(v_{0},Q_{0}))+Q_{0}-\underline{\smash{\mu}}(\kappa(v_{0},Q_{0}))\Big)=(v_{0},Q_{0}).

Therefore
\kappa
is transverse to
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(G,V,W)
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
3.15
.

Notice that on the open subset

M^{d}(G,V,W)_{H}^{+}\subseteq M^{d}(G,V,W)

the action stratification coming from the
G
-action coincides with the action stratification coming from the
H
-action, as all the strata on both sides are indexed by
V
-essential subgroups of
H
. Denote by

\mathcal{WS}^{\infty}(Z^{d}(G,V,W)_{H}^{+};H)\subseteq\mathcal{WS}^{\infty}(Z^{d}(G,V,W)_{H}^{+})

the set of smooth Whitney stratifications which respect the action stratification. Similarly, one has the corresponding set
\mathcal{WS}^{\infty}(Z^{d}(H,V,W)_{H}^{+};H)
of Whitney stratifications. Then all the Whitney stratifications contained in the statement of Proposition
3.15
belong to the corresponding subsets. Then by Lemma
3.31
,
3.32
and Lemma
B.6
, it follows that

\mu^{*}{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(H,V,W)|_{Z^{d}(G,V,W)_{H}^{+}}\equiv{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(G,V,W)|_{Z^{d}(G,V,W)_{H}^{+}}.

Moreover, as both
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(H,V,W)
and
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(G,V,W)|_{Z^{d}(G,V,W)_{H}^{+}}
have connected strata, one has

\mu^{*}{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(H,V,W)|_{Z^{d}(G,V,W)_{H}^{+}}={\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(G,V,W)|_{Z^{d}(G,V,W)_{H}^{+}}.

Then consider the induced map

\mu_{*}:{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(G,V,W)|_{Z^{d}(G,V,W)_{H}^{+}}\to{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(H,V,W)|_{Z^{d}(G,V,W)_{H}^{+}}.

Using an average map
\kappa
provided by Lemma
3.30
and its property provided by Lemma
3.31
, we see
\kappa_{*}
is an inverse of
\mu_{*}
. Lastly, since all arrows of the diagram (
3.3
) are induced by pullbacks of Whitney stratifications and pullback is functorial, the diagram (
3.3
) commutes.
∎

3.7.1.
Proof of Lemma
3.30

Lemma 3.33
.

Denote
d_{0}=|G|
. There exists an
H
-invariant smooth map

V_{H}^{+}\to{\rm Poly}_{H}^{d_{0}}(V,{\mathbb{C}})

denoted by
v\mapsto L_{v}
, such that

L_{v}(\tau v)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}1,&\ {\rm if}\ \tau\in H,\\
0,&\ {\rm if}\ \tau\notin H.\end{array}\right.

(3.8)

Proof.

We first construct a map
v\mapsto L_{v}^{\prime\prime}\in{\rm Poly}^{d_{0}}(V,{\mathbb{C}})
satisfying (
3.8
) without the
H
-invariance condition. For each
v\in V_{H}^{+}
, because
G_{v}
is contained in
H
,
\tau_{1}v\neq\tau_{2}v
if
\tau_{1}\in H
and
\tau_{2}\notin H
. Consider a 1-dimensional subspace
S_{v}\subseteq V
and a complement
S_{v}^{\bot}
. Let
p_{v}:V\to S_{v}
be the projection induced from the splitting
V\cong S_{v}\oplus S_{v}^{\bot}
. One can choose
S_{v}
and
S_{v}^{\bot}
such that for a small open neighborhood
O(v)\subseteq V_{H}^{+}
of
v
,

p_{v}(\tau_{1}v^{\prime})\neq p_{v}(\tau_{2}v^{\prime})\ {\rm if\ }\tau_{1}\in H,\ \tau_{2}\notin H,\ v^{\prime}\in O(v).

Then by the Lagrange interpolation method, there exists a complex polynomial
f_{v,v^{\prime}}:S_{v}\to{\mathbb{C}}
(given by the exact formula of Lagrange interpolation) of degree at most
d_{0}=|G|
such that

f_{v,v^{\prime}}(p_{v}(\tau v^{\prime}))=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}1,&\tau\in H,\\
0,&\tau\notin H.\end{array}\right.

Then define the polynomial

L_{v,v^{\prime}}:=f_{v,v^{\prime}}\circ p_{v}:V\to{\mathbb{C}},\ \forall v^{\prime}\in O(v).

Then
L_{v,v^{\prime}}(\tau v^{\prime})=1
if
\tau\in H
and
L_{v,v^{\prime}}(\tau v^{\prime})=0
otherwise.

For each
v\in V_{H}^{+}
, we can find as above an open neighborhood
O(v)
and a function
v^{\prime}\mapsto L_{v,v^{\prime}}\in{\rm Poly}^{d_{0}}(V,{\mathbb{C}})
. As
V_{H}^{+}
is Lindelöf, one can cover it by a countable subcover
O(v_{i})
,
i=1,\ldots
. Find a subordinate partition of unity
\rho_{i}
and define

v\mapsto L_{v}^{\prime\prime}:=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\rho_{i}(v)L_{v_{i},v}.

The local finiteness of the partition of unity implies that this is a smooth map from
V_{H}^{+}
to
{\rm Poly}^{d_{0}}(V,{\mathbb{C}})
. Moreover,

L_{v}^{\prime\prime}(\tau v^{\prime})|_{v^{\prime}=v}=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\rho_{i}(v)L_{v_{i},v}(\tau v^{\prime})|_{v^{\prime}=v}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}1,&\tau\in H,\\
0,&\tau\notin H\end{array}\right.

Then define

L_{v}^{\prime}:=\frac{1}{|H|}\sum_{\tau\in H}L_{v}^{\prime\prime}\circ\tau\in{\rm Poly}_{H}^{d_{0}}(V,{\mathbb{C}})

which still satisfies (
3.8
), and define

L_{v}:=\frac{1}{|H|}\sum_{\tau\in H}L_{\tau v}^{\prime}\in{\rm Poly}_{H}^{d_{0}}(V,{\mathbb{C}})

so the map
v\mapsto L_{v}
is
H
-invariant satisfying (
3.8
).
∎

Now we define a map

\begin{split}\underline{\smash{\kappa}}^{\prime}:M^{d}(V,H,W)_{H}^{+}&\to{\rm Poly}_{G}^{d+d_{0}}(V,W)\\
(v,Q)&\mapsto\frac{1}{|H|}\sum_{\tau\in G}\tau^{-1}\circ\big(L_{v}Q\big)\circ\tau.\end{split}

Define

\kappa^{\prime}(v,Q)=(v,\underline{\smash{\kappa}}^{\prime}(v,Q))\in M^{d+d_{0}}(G,V,W)_{H}^{+}.

On the other hand, when
d\geq d_{1}
, Lemma
3.24
provides a
G
-invariant holomorphic map

\rho:V\times{\rm Poly}_{G}^{d+d_{0}}(V,W)\to V\times{\rm Poly}_{G}^{d}(V,W)

satisfying the two conditions of Lemma
3.24
. Then define

\kappa:=\rho\circ\kappa^{\prime}:M^{d}(H,V,W)_{H}^{+}\to M^{d}(G,V,W)_{H}^{+}.

As both
\rho
and
\kappa^{\prime}
preserve the evaluation map.
\kappa
is an averaging map. This concludes the proof of Lemma
3.30
.

3.7.2.
More preparations for Lemma
3.31

Lemma 3.34
.

Let
(G,F,E)
be a family NC triple over
X
(Definition
2.45
). Let
\phi:M^{d}(G,F,E)\to M^{d}(G,F,E)
be a fiberwise diffeomorphism which preserves the action stratification and which preserves the subbundle
Z^{d}(G,F,E)
set-wise. Then
\phi^{*}{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(G,F,E)={\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(G,F,E)
.

Proof.

Notice that the fiberwise
G
-action gives a family version of the action stratification
{\mathfrak{M}}
on
M^{d}(G,F,E)
.
We first observe that
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(G,F,E)
is a minimal Whitney stratification which respects
{\mathfrak{M}}
(Definition
3.4
). Indeed, as the bundles are locally trivial, the restriction of
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(G,F,E)
to the preimage to any small open subset
U\subseteq X
is minimal among all Whitney stratifications which respects
{\mathfrak{M}}
. As the minimality is defined via the comparison of the dimension filtration, which is a local condition, the minimality of
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(G,F,E)
follows. Then by Proposition
B.15
, it follows that
\phi^{*}{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(G,F,E)\equiv{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(G,F,E)
. Moreover, when
X
is connected,
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(G,F,E)
has connected strata; as
\kappa
is a diffeomorphism, the pullback
\phi^{*}{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(G,F,E)
also has connected strata over each connected component of
X
. Hence one has the equality as claimed.
∎

Recall that for any lift
{\mathfrak{p}}:F\to\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(F,E)

of an NC bundle map
S\in C_{G}^{\rm NC}(F,E)
(see Definition
2.46
), its graph is the subset

{\rm graph}({\mathfrak{p}})=\{(v,{\mathfrak{p}}(v))\ |\ v\in F\}\subseteq M^{d}(G,F,E).

Parker
[
Par13
]
observed the following. Two maps
{\mathfrak{p}}_{1},{\mathfrak{p}}_{2}:F\to\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(F,E)
are lifts of the same bundle map
S\in C_{G}^{\rm NC}(F,E)
if and only if

{\rm graph}({\mathfrak{p}}_{1}-{\mathfrak{p}}_{2})\subseteq Z^{d}(G,F,E).

We reprove a result of Parker
[
Par13
]
.

Lemma 3.35
.

(cf.
[
Par13
, Lemma 4.10]
)
Let
(G,F,E)
be as above. Fix an integer
d>0
. Let
F^{\prime}\subseteq F
be an open subset of the total space
F
and let
{\mathfrak{p}}_{1},{\mathfrak{p}}_{2}:F^{\prime}\to\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(F,E)
be two smooth bundle maps such that

{\rm graph}({\mathfrak{p}}_{1}-{\mathfrak{p}}_{2})\subseteq Z^{d}(G,F,E).

Then
{\rm graph}({\mathfrak{p}}_{1})
is transverse
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(G,F,E)
if and only if
{\rm graph}({\mathfrak{p}}_{2})
is transverse to
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(G,F,E)
. Moreover, in this case, the two Whitney stratifications on
S_{{\mathfrak{p}}_{1}}^{-1}(0)=S_{{\mathfrak{p}}_{2}}^{-1}(0)
pulled back by
{\rm graph}({\mathfrak{p}}_{1})
and by
{\rm graph}({\mathfrak{p}}_{2})
coincide.

Proof.

Suppose the graph of
{\mathfrak{p}}_{1}
is transverse to
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(G,F,E)
. For any
(v_{0},{\mathfrak{p}}_{2}(v_{0}))\in Z^{d}(G,F,E)
, we would like to show that the graph of
{\mathfrak{p}}_{2}
is transverse to
Z^{d}(G,F,E)
at this point
(v_{0},{\mathfrak{p}}_{2}(v_{0}))
. Choose a compactly supported cut-off function

\rho_{0}:F^{\prime}\to[0,1]

which is identically
1
near
v_{0}
. Consider the smooth vertical vector field on the total space of the vector bundle
F\oplus\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(F,E)
defined by

f(v,P)=\big(0,\rho_{0}(v)({\mathfrak{p}}_{1}(v)-{\mathfrak{p}}_{2}(v))\big).

(3.9)

Accordingly, the flow of
f
is the 1-parameter family of fiber-preserving diffeomorphisms

\phi_{t}(v,P)=\big(v,P+t\rho_{0}(v)({\mathfrak{p}}_{1}(v)-{\mathfrak{p}}_{2}(v))\big)

of
M^{d}(G,F,E)
which exists for all time
t
. It is also easy to see that
\phi_{t}
preserves the action stratification on
M^{d}(G,F,E)
and the set
Z^{d}(G,F,E)
. Hence by Lemma
3.34
,
\phi_{t}
pulls back the canonical Whitney stratification on
Z^{d}(G,F,E)
to itself. Moreover,
\phi_{1}
maps a neighborhood of
(v_{0},{\mathfrak{p}}_{2}(v_{0}))
in
{\rm graph}({\mathfrak{p}}_{2})
to a neighborhood of
(v_{0},{\mathfrak{p}}_{1}(v_{0}))
in
{\rm graph}({\mathfrak{p}}_{1})
. Hence the graph of
{\mathfrak{p}}_{2}
is also transverse to
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}
at
v_{0}
.

To show that the two pullback Whitney stratifications agree, we just need to check locally around a point
v_{0}\in{\rm graph}({\mathfrak{p}}_{1})^{-1}(Z^{d})={\rm graph}({\mathfrak{p}}_{2})^{-1}(Z^{d})\subseteq F^{\prime}
. Let
\phi_{1}
be the time-1 map of the flow of the vector field (
3.9
). Then in a neighborhood of
v_{0}\in F^{\prime}
, as maps, one has

{\rm graph}({\mathfrak{p}}_{1})=\phi_{1}\circ{\rm graph}({\mathfrak{p}}_{2}).

On the other hand, as
\phi_{1}
preserves the canonical Whitney stratification
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}
, it follows that near
v_{0}
,
{\rm graph}({\mathfrak{p}}_{1})^{*}{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}={\rm graph}({\mathfrak{p}}_{2})^{*}\phi_{1}^{*}{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}={\rm graph}({\mathfrak{p}}_{2})^{*}{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}
.
∎

3.7.3.
Proof of Lemma
3.31

Consider a family NC triple
(G,F,E)
over the base
X=\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(V,W)
with

\displaystyle\ F:=X\times V,\

\displaystyle\ E:=X\times W.

Consider the subbundle

F_{H}^{+}:=X\times V_{H}^{+}\subseteq F.

Then
\kappa\circ\mu
can be viewed as a bundle map

\begin{split}{\mathfrak{p}}:F_{H}^{+}&\ \to\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(F,E)\cong X\times{\rm Poly}_{G}^{d}(V,W)\\
(P,v)&\mapsto\Big(P,\underline{\smash{\kappa}}(\mu(v,P))\Big)\end{split}

On the other hand, there is another natural bundle map

\begin{split}{\mathfrak{p}}_{0}:F_{H}^{+}&\ \to\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(F,E),\\
(P,v)&\ \mapsto(P,P).\end{split}

Then the construction implies that

{\rm graph}({\mathfrak{p}}-{\mathfrak{p}}_{0})\subseteq Z^{d}(G,F,E).

As
{\rm graph}({\mathfrak{p}}_{0})
is transverse to the canonical Whitney stratification on
Z^{d}(G,F,E)
, by Lemma
3.35
the graph of
{\mathfrak{p}}
is also transverse to it. Equivalently, it means
\kappa\circ\mu
is transverse to
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(G,V,W)
. As
{\rm graph}({\mathfrak{p}}_{0})
pulls back the canonical Whitney stratification on
Z^{d}(G,F,E)
to itself, it is the same for
{\rm graph}({\mathfrak{p}}_{1})
. Equivalently, this means
(\kappa\circ\mu)^{*}{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(G,V,W)={\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(G,V,W)|_{Z^{d}(G,V,W)_{H}^{+}}
.

For the case of
\mu\circ\kappa
, consider another family NC triple
(H,\tilde{F},\tilde{E})
over the base
Y=\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{H}^{d}(V,W)
with

\displaystyle\ \tilde{F}:=Y\times V,\

\displaystyle\ \tilde{E}:=Y\times W.

Then there is a subbundle

\tilde{F}_{H}^{+}:=Y\times V_{H}^{+}\subseteq F.

Then
\mu\circ\kappa
can be viewed as a bundle map

\begin{split}\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}:\tilde{F}_{H}^{+}&\ \to\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{H}^{d}(\tilde{F},\tilde{E})\\
(Q,v)&\ \mapsto\Big(Q,\underline{\smash{\mu}}(\kappa(v,Q))\Big).\end{split}

There is another bundle map

\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}_{0}(Q,v)=(Q,Q).

Then
{\rm graph}(\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}-\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}_{0})\subseteq Z^{d}(H,\tilde{F},\tilde{E})
. Then by Lemma
3.35
,
{\rm graph}(\tilde{\mathfrak{q}})
is transverse to
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(H,\tilde{F},\tilde{E})
and pulls it back to the canonical Whitney stratification on
\tilde{F}_{H}^{+}
. It is equivalent to that
\mu\circ\kappa
is transverse to
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(H,V,W)
and pulls it back to
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(H,V,W)
over
Z^{d}(H,V,W)_{H}^{+}
.

4.
FOP Transverse Sections

In this section we define the space of FOP transverse sections and prove Theorem
1.1
. Roughly speaking, a normally complex section is FOP transverse if it induces a certain map that is transverse to the variety
Z
equipped with the canonical Whitney stratification specified in the previous section. The properties of the canonical Whitney stratifications allow us to deduce properties of FOP transverse sections. Within this section, let
({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}})
be an NC pair (Definition
2.17
), i.e., a normally complex vector bundle
{\mathcal{E}}
over a normally complex orbifold
{\mathcal{U}}
.

4.1.
FOP transverse bundle maps

We first discuss the FOP transversality on the local model. Let
(G,F,E)
be a family NC triple over a smooth manifold
X
. Recall that one has the space

C_{G}^{\rm NC}(F,E)\subseteq C_{G}^{\infty}(F,E)

of normally complex bundle maps (Definition
2.45
).

Definition 4.1
.

Consider an NC bundle map
S\in C_{G}^{\rm NC}(F,E)
and a lift
{\mathfrak{p}}:F\to\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(F,E)
(Definition
2.46
).

(1)

The lift
{\mathfrak{p}}
is said to be
FOP transverse
at
(x,v)\in F
if
d\geq d_{1}(G,V,W)
of Proposition
3.14
where
V
and
W
are fibers of
F
and
E
respectively, and if the graph
{\rm graph}({\mathfrak{p}})\subseteq M^{d}(G,F,E)
is transverse to the canonical Whitney stratification of
Z^{d}(G,F,E)
at
(x,v,{\mathfrak{p}}(x,v))
.

(2)

S
is said to be
FOP transverse
at
(x,v)\in F
if there exists a lift
{\mathfrak{p}}
which is FOP transverse at
(x,v)
.

We show that the transversality condition is independent of the lift.

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
{\mathfrak{p}}:F\to\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(F,E)
and
{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}:F\to\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d^{\prime}}(F,E)
be two lifts of
S:F\to E
, then
{\mathfrak{p}}
is FOP transverse at
(x,v)
if and only if
{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}
is FOP transverse at
(x,v)
.

Proof.

If
d\leq d^{\prime}
, we may identify
{\mathfrak{p}}
with a map
{\mathfrak{q}}:F\to\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d^{\prime}}(F,E)
. Then by Proposition
3.14
,
{\mathfrak{p}}
is transverse to
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(G,F,E)
at
(x,v)
if and only if
{\mathfrak{q}}
is transverse to
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d^{\prime}}(G,F,E)
at
(x,v)
. Hence we may assume
d^{\prime}=d
. Moreover, as
{\mathfrak{p}}
and
{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}
induce the same section
S
, it follows that
{\rm graph}({\mathfrak{p}}-{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime})\subseteq Z^{d}(G,F,E)
. Then by Lemma
3.35
, the two transversality conditions are equivalent.
∎

Lemma 4.3
.

The set of points
(x,v)\in F
at which an NC bundle map
S\in C_{G}^{\rm NC}(F,E)
is FOP transverse is open in
F
.

Proof.

Choose a lift
{\mathfrak{p}}:F\to\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(F,E)
for a sufficiently large
d
. Consider the intersection between
{\rm graph}({\mathfrak{p}})
and
Z^{d}(G,F,E)
. Trotman
[
Tro78
]
proved that the set of transverse points to a Whitney stratified subset is open. Therefore, the set of points on
{\rm graph}({\mathfrak{p}})
where the intersection is transverse is open. As
{\rm graph}({\mathfrak{p}})
is homeomorphic to
F
, the lemma follows.
∎

Another crucial feature is related to the change of group. Recall that if
H\subseteq_{F}G
is an essential subgroup, then there is the basic decomposition

F=F_{H}\oplus\check{F}_{H}

and the total space
F
is canonically identified with the total space of
\pi_{F_{H}}^{*}\check{F}_{H}\to F_{H}
. Then consider the canonical map defined in the proof of Lemma
2.44

\mu:M^{d}(G,F,E)\to M^{d}(H,F,E)

(4.1)

which induces a natural map between spaces of NC bundle maps, which, by abuse of notation, was denoted by

\mu:C_{G}^{\rm NC}(F,E)\to C_{H}^{\rm NC}(\pi_{F_{H}}^{*}\check{F}_{H},\pi_{F_{H}}^{*}E).

Lemma 4.4
.

Suppose
S
is FOP transverse at a point
(x,v)
whose stabilizer is contained in
H
, then
\mu(S)
is FOP transverse at
(x,v)\in F
.

Proof.

Choose a lift
{\mathfrak{p}}:F\to\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(F,E)
of
S
. Using the map (
4.1
) one obtains an
H
-invariant bundle map
{\mathfrak{q}}:F\to\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{H}^{d}(F,E)
so that

\mu({\rm graph}({\mathfrak{p}}))={\rm graph}({\mathfrak{q}}).

Since the stabilizer of
(x,v)
is contained in
H
, using the family version (which is a trivial extension) of Proposition
3.15
, one can see that
{\rm graph}({\mathfrak{q}})
is transverse to
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(H,F,E)
at
(x,v,{\mathfrak{q}}(x,v))
. Therefore, as an
H
-equivariant NC bundle map,
S
is FOP transverse at
(x,v)
.

On the other hand, under the natural identification

M^{d}(H,F,E)\cong M^{d}(H,\pi_{F_{H}}^{*}\check{F}_{H},\pi_{F_{H}}^{*}E)

{\mathfrak{q}}
becomes a lift of the (same)
H
-equivariant bundle map
S:\pi_{F_{H}}^{*}\check{F}_{H}\to\pi_{F_{H}}^{*}E
and the Whitney stratification
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(H,F,E)
is identical to
{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(H,\pi_{F_{H}}^{*}\check{F}_{H},\pi_{F_{H}}^{*}E)
. Therefore,
{\rm graph}({\mathfrak{q}})
is transverse to the latter at the corresponding point and hence
\mu(S)
is FOP transverse at
(x,v)
.
∎

Next we examine more carefully the FOP transversality condition aiming at proving the existence and density of transverse elements. Let
G,F,E
be as before. Let
{\rm proj}_{2}:M^{d}(G,F,E)\to\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(F,E)
be the vector bundle projection.

Lemma 4.5
.

Suppose
F_{G}=0
(so
F
is a complex vector bundle). Then the graph of
{\mathfrak{p}}:F\to\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(F,E)
is transverse to a stratum
Z_{\alpha}\in{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(G,F,E)
at a point
(x,0)\in X\subseteq F
if and only
{\mathfrak{p}}|_{X}:X\to\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(F,E)
is transverse to the smooth map
{\rm proj}_{2}:Z_{\alpha}\to\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(F,E)
at
x
.

Proof.

We may assume that
F
and
E
are trivial with fibers
V
and
W
respectively. Then
{\mathfrak{p}}
is equivalent to a
G
-invariant map

{\mathfrak{p}}:X\times V\to\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(V,W).

Given
(x,0)\in X\subseteq F
, the tangent space of
{\rm graph}({\mathfrak{p}})
at the corresponding point is

\Big\{\Big(\eta,\xi,D_{x}{\mathfrak{p}}(x,0)(\eta)+D_{v}{\mathfrak{p}}(x,0)(\xi)\Big)\ |\ \xi\in V,\ \eta\in T_{x}X\Big\}\subseteq T_{x}X\oplus V\oplus\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(V,W).

As
V
contains no trivial representations and
{\mathfrak{p}}
is
G
-invariant, the partial derivative
D_{v}{\mathfrak{p}}
vanishes along the zero section. Hence the above tangent space to
{\rm graph}({\mathfrak{p}})
is
T_{x}X\oplus V\oplus{\rm Im}(D_{x}{\mathfrak{p}})
. As
{\rm proj}_{2}
collapses the
V
-direction, the transversality is equivalent to the transversality of
{\rm Im}(D_{x}{\mathfrak{p}})
to
{\rm proj}_{2}:Z_{\alpha}\to\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(V,W)
.
∎

Lemma 4.6
.

Suppose
F_{G}=0
and
{\mathfrak{p}}_{0}:F\to\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(F,E)
is FOP transverse near a closed subset
Y\subseteq F
, then there exists
{\mathfrak{p}}:F\to\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(F,E)
which is FOP transverse near the base
X
such that
{\mathfrak{p}}={\mathfrak{p}}_{0}
near
Y
. Moreover, if we equip
\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(F,E)
with a norm and are given
\delta>0
, then one can require that
\|{\mathfrak{p}}-{\mathfrak{p}}_{0}\|\leq\delta
.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.5
,
{\mathfrak{p}}_{0}|_{X}
is transverse to
{\rm proj}_{2}:Z_{\alpha}\to\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(F,E)
near
Y\cap X
for each stratum
Z_{\alpha}\in{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(G,F,E)
. Then the standard transversality result shows that there exists a map
{\mathfrak{p}}_{1}:X\to\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(F,E)
which is transverse to
{\rm proj}_{2}:Z_{\alpha}\to\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(F,E)
for all
\alpha
, such that
{\mathfrak{p}}_{1}
agrees with
{\mathfrak{p}}_{0}|_{X}
near
Y\cap X
and such that
{\mathfrak{p}}_{1}
is sufficiently close to
{\mathfrak{p}}_{0}|_{X}
. Consider the pullback
\pi_{F}^{*}{\mathfrak{p}}_{1}:F\to\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(F,E)
; by abuse of notation, still denote it by
{\mathfrak{p}}_{1}
.

Now choose a pair of
G
-invariant neighborhoods
O^{\prime}\subseteq O^{\prime\prime}
of
Y
such that the closure of
O^{\prime}
is contained in
O^{\prime\prime}
and such that
{\mathfrak{p}}_{0}
is FOP transverse in
O^{\prime\prime}
. Choose a
G
-invariant cut-off function
\rho:F\to[0,1]
supported in a sufficiently small neighborhood of
X\setminus O^{\prime}\subseteq F\setminus Y
which is also identically
1
near
X\setminus O^{\prime\prime}
. Define

{\mathfrak{p}}=(1-\rho){\mathfrak{p}}_{0}+\rho{\mathfrak{p}}_{1}:F\to\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(F,E)

which agrees with
{\mathfrak{p}}_{0}
near
Y\subseteq F
and which is sufficiently close to
{\mathfrak{p}}_{0}
. Moreover, its restriction to the zero section is transverse to each stratum of
Z^{d}(G,F,E)
under
{\rm proj}_{2}
. Then by Lemma
4.5
,
{\mathfrak{p}}
is FOP transverse at each point of
X
. By Lemma
4.3
, it is FOP transverse near
X
.
∎

4.2.
FOP transverse sections

Suppose
({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}})
is equipped with a straightening
({\mathcal{U}}^{\#},{\mathcal{E}}^{\#})
(Definition
2.35
). Let
\hat{C}=(G,U,E,\hat{\psi})
be a bundle chart for
{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{U}}
. Then for each
U
-essential subgroup
H\subseteq G
, near
U_{H}
a section
S:U\to E
can be identified with a nonlinear bundle map

S_{H}^{\#}:N^{\epsilon}U_{H}\to E|_{U_{H}}.

Definition 4.7
.

We say that a
G
-equivariant section
S:U\to E
is
FOP transverse at
x\in U
if it is an NC section in a neighborhood of
x
and if the NC bundle map

S_{G_{x}}^{\#}:N^{\epsilon}U_{G_{x}}\to E|_{U_{G_{x}}}

is FOP transverse at
(x,0)
.
S
is said to be
FOP transverse
if it is FOP transverse at all points.

It is easy to see that the FOP transversality condition is invariant under chart embeddings.

Lemma 4.8
.

Let
\hat{C}_{i}=(G_{i},U_{i},E_{i},\hat{\psi}_{i})
,
i=1,2
be two overlapping bundle charts of
{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{U}}
such that
{\mathcal{S}}
is pulled back to equivariant sections
S_{i}:U_{i}\to E_{i}
. Suppose
\psi_{1}(x_{1})=\psi_{2}(x_{2})
. Then
S_{1}
is FOP transverse at
x_{1}
if and only if
S_{2}
is FOP transverse at
x_{2}
.

Proof.

It follows from the invariance property of the canonical Whitney stratification provided by Proposition
3.13
.
∎

Lemma 4.9
.

Let
S:U\to E
be a
G
-equivariant NC section. Then the set of points
x\in U
where
S
is FOP transverse is open.

Proof.

We may assume
G_{x}=G
. Using the straightening, we may assume
S
is an NC bundle map
S_{G}^{\#}\in C_{G}^{\rm NC}(F,E)
with
F=NU_{G}
and
x
is a point in the base
X=U_{G}
. Let
{\mathfrak{p}}:F\to\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(F,E)
be a lift of
S_{G}^{\#}
. Then by definition,
{\rm graph}({\mathfrak{p}})\subseteq M^{d}(G,F,E)
is transverse to
Z^{d}(G,F,E)
at
(x,0,{\mathfrak{p}}(x,0))
. By Trotman’s theorem, they are transverse at all nearby points
(y,v)
. If
H=G_{v}\subseteq G
, then the map
\mu
in (
4.1
) changes
{\mathfrak{p}}
to an
H
-invariant lift
{\mathfrak{q}}
of
S_{G}^{\#}
near
(y,v)
. By Lemma
4.4
,
S_{G}^{\#}
is FOP transverse at
(y,v)
. Therefore, the set of FOP transverse points is open.
∎

We can then prove the chartwise existence and
C^{0}
-density of FOP transverse sections.

Proposition 4.10
.

Let
\hat{C}=(G,U,E,\hat{\psi})
be a bundle chart of
{\mathcal{E}}
and
S_{0}:U\to E
be an NC section (with respect to the fixed straightening), then for each
\delta>0
, there exists an FOP transverse section
S:U\to E
such that
\|S-S_{0}\|_{C^{0}}\leq\delta
. Moreover, if
Y\subseteq U
is a
G
-invariant closed subset and
S
is FOP transverse near
Y
, then
S
can be chosen to agree with
S_{0}
near
Y
.

Proof.

Via an induction on essential subgroups, one can see it suffices to prove the following statement. Let
H\subseteq G
be a maximal
U
-essential subgroup. Then there exists an NC section
S:U\to E
which is FOP transverse near
Y\cup GU_{H}
and which agrees with
S_{0}
near
Y
. To prove this statement, first choose a
G
-invariant

\epsilon:GU_{H}\to{\mathbb{R}}_{+}.

As
H
is a maximal
U
-essential subgroup, it is possible to choose
\epsilon
such that for any
H^{\prime}
conjugate to
H
,

|N^{\epsilon}U_{H}|\cap|N^{\epsilon}U_{H^{\prime}}|\neq\emptyset\Longrightarrow H=H^{\prime}.

Next, consider the induced NC bundle map

S_{0,H}^{\#}:N^{\epsilon}U_{H}\to E|_{U_{H}}.

Choose a lift

{\mathfrak{p}}_{0}:N^{\epsilon}U_{H}\to\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{H}^{d}(NU_{H},E|_{U_{H}}).

Then by Lemma
4.6
, there exists

{\mathfrak{p}}_{1}:N^{\epsilon}U_{H}\to\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{H}^{d}(NU_{H},E|_{U_{H}})

which is sufficiently close to
{\mathfrak{p}}_{0}
, agrees with
{\mathfrak{p}}_{0}
near
Y\cap|N^{\epsilon}U_{H}|
, and is FOP transverse near
U_{H}
. Let
S_{{\mathfrak{p}}_{1}}:N^{\epsilon}U_{H}\to E|_{|N^{\epsilon}U_{H}|}
be the corresponding section defined in the tubular neighborhood
|N^{\epsilon}U_{H}|
of
U_{H}
. Via
G
-action, the lift
{\mathfrak{p}}_{1}
induces sections defined on
|N^{\epsilon}U_{H^{\prime}}|
for all
H^{\prime}
conjugate to
H
such that the totality defines a
G
-equivariant NC section

S_{1}:G|N^{\epsilon}U_{H}|\to E|_{G|N^{\epsilon}U_{H}|}.

Now choose a
G
-invariant cut-off function
\rho:U\to[0,1]
supported near
GU_{H}
. Consider the section

S=(1-\rho)S_{0}+\rho S_{1}.

Then by Lemma
2.51
,
S
is an NC section. Moreover,
S
is FOP transverse near
GU_{H}
and agrees with
S_{0}
near
Y
.
∎

4.3.
Proof of Theorem

1.1

4.3.1.
FOP transverse sections on orbifolds, classical transversality, and locality

We first formally define the space of FOP transverse sections.

Definition 4.11
.

Let
{\mathcal{U}}
be an NC orbifold (without boundary) and
{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{U}}
be an NC vector bundle. Recall the definition of straightenings (Definition
2.35
).

(1)

Let
({\mathcal{U}}^{\#},{\mathcal{E}}^{\#})
be a straightening of
({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}})
, an NC section
{\mathcal{S}}\in\Gamma^{\rm NC}({\mathcal{U}}^{\#},{\mathcal{E}}^{\#})
is called
FOP transverse
if for each bundle chart
\hat{C}=(G,U,E,\hat{\psi})
, the pullback section
S:U\to E
is FOP transverse (Definition
4.7
).

(2)

Define
\Gamma^{\rm FOP}({\mathcal{U}}^{\#},{\mathcal{E}}^{\#})\subseteq\Gamma^{\rm NC}({\mathcal{U}}^{\#},{\mathcal{E}}^{\#})
to be the subset of sections which are FOP transverse.

(3)

By taking the union over all straightenings on
({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}})
, define

\Gamma^{\rm FOP}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}})=\bigcup_{({\mathcal{U}}^{\#},{\mathcal{E}}^{\#})}\Gamma^{\rm FOP}({\mathcal{U}}^{\#},{\mathcal{E}}^{\#})

and call its elements
FOP transverse sections
of
{\mathcal{E}}
.

The
(Classical Transversality)
and
(Locality)
stated in Theorem
1.1
are straightforward. If
{\mathcal{U}}
is a manifold, then there is a canonical (trivial) NC structure and all smooth sections are NC sections. The equivalence between FOP transversality and classical transversality can be traced via the definition. On the other hand, if
{\mathcal{S}}\in\Gamma^{\rm FOP}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}})
, then it is FOP transverse with respect to a certain straightening
({\mathcal{U}}^{\#},{\mathcal{E}}^{\#})
. The straightening restricts to one over any open subset
{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}
. As the FOP transversality condition is defined pointwise, it follows that
{\mathcal{S}}|_{{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}}\in\Gamma^{\rm FOP}({\mathcal{U}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{E}}|_{{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}})
.

4.3.2.
Density of FOP transverse sections and Extension Property I

Proposition 4.12
.

Given a smooth section
{\mathcal{S}}_{0}\in\Gamma({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}})
which is FOP transverse near a closed subset
{\mathcal{Y}}\subseteq{\mathcal{U}}
, for for any
\delta>0
, there exists an FOP transverse section
{\mathcal{S}}:{\mathcal{U}}\to{\mathcal{E}}
which agrees with
{\mathcal{S}}_{0}
near
{\mathcal{Y}}
such that
\|{\mathcal{S}}_{0}-{\mathcal{S}}\|_{C^{0}}\leq\delta
.

Proof.

By definition, there exists a straightening near
{\mathcal{Y}}
with respect to which
{\mathcal{S}}_{0}
is normally complex and FOP transverse. By Corollary
2.37
, there exists a straightening
({\mathcal{U}}^{\#},{\mathcal{E}}^{\#})
of
({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}})
which agrees with the existing one near
{\mathcal{Y}}
. As normally complex sections form a submodule over
C^{\infty}({\mathcal{U}})
, by using a cut-off function, we may assume that
{\mathcal{S}}_{0}
is normally complex with respect to this straightening by the density property stated in Proposition
2.52
. For the rest of the proof, we will use this straightening.

On the other hand, as
|{\mathcal{U}}|
is Lindelöf and paracompact, one can find a countable and locally finite cover of
|{\mathcal{U}}|
by bundle charts
\hat{C}_{i}=(G_{i},U_{i},E_{i},\hat{\psi}_{i})
,
i=1,\ldots,\infty
. We may also find precompact
G_{i}
-invariant open subsets
U_{i}^{\prime}\subseteq U_{i}
such that
\{\psi_{i}(U_{i}^{\prime})\}
still covers
{\mathcal{U}}
. We prove the following statement by induction on
k=1,2,\ldots
.

•

For each
k
, there exists a section
{\mathcal{S}}_{k}\in\Gamma^{\rm NC}({\mathcal{U}}^{\#},{\mathcal{E}}^{\#})
such that
{\mathcal{S}}_{k}
is FOP transverse near
\bigcup_{i\leq k}\psi_{i}(\overline{U_{i}^{\prime}})
and which agrees with
{\mathcal{S}}_{0}
near
{\mathcal{Y}}
. Moreover

\|{\mathcal{S}}_{k}-{\mathcal{S}}_{0}\|_{C^{0}}\leq\left(1-\frac{1}{2^{k}}\right)\delta.

For the
k=1
case, let
S_{0,1}:U_{1}\to E_{1}
be the pullback of
{\mathcal{S}}_{0}
to the chart
\hat{C}_{1}
. Then by Proposition
4.10
, there exists
S_{1}^{\prime}:U_{1}\to E_{1}
which is FOP transverse on
U_{1}
and which agrees with
S_{0,1}
near
Y_{1}:=\psi_{1}^{-1}({\mathcal{Y}})
such that
\|S_{1}^{\prime}-S_{0,1}\|_{C^{0}}\leq\frac{\delta}{2}
. Choose a
G_{1}
-invariant cut-off function
\rho_{1}:U_{1}\to[0,1]
supported near
\overline{U_{1}^{\prime}}
such that
\rho_{1}|_{U_{1}^{\prime}}\equiv 1
. Regarding
\rho_{1}
as a function on
{\mathcal{U}}
, then
\rho_{1}S_{1}^{\prime}
defines a normally complex section of
{\mathcal{E}}
. Then define

{\mathcal{S}}_{1}=(1-\rho_{1}){\mathcal{S}}_{0}+\rho_{1}S_{1}^{\prime}

which is FOP transverse near
\psi_{1}(\overline{U_{1}^{\prime}})
and which agrees with
{\mathcal{S}}_{0}
near
{\mathcal{Y}}
. Moreover,

\|{\mathcal{S}}_{1}-{\mathcal{S}}_{0}\|_{C^{0}}\leq\frac{\delta}{2}.

Suppose we have proved the above statement for
k-1
. Denote

\displaystyle\ {\mathcal{Y}}_{k}:={\mathcal{Y}}\cup\bigcup_{i\leq k-1}\psi_{i}(\overline{U_{i}^{\prime}})\subseteq{\mathcal{Y}},\

\displaystyle\ Y_{k}:=\psi_{k}^{-1}({\mathcal{Y}}_{k})\subseteq U_{k}.

The latter is a
G_{k}
-invariant closed subset. Let
S_{k-1,k}:U_{k}\to E_{k}
be the pullback of
{\mathcal{S}}_{k-1}
to
\hat{C}_{k}
. Then
S_{k-1,k}
is FOP transverse near
Y_{k}
. Then by Proposition
4.10
, there exists an FOP transverse section
S_{k}^{\prime}:U_{k}\to E_{k}
which agrees with
S_{k-1,k}
near
Y_{k}
such that

\|S_{k}^{\prime}-S_{k-1,k}\|_{C^{0}}\leq\frac{\delta}{2^{k}}.

Choose a
G_{k}
-invariant cut-off function
\rho_{k}:U_{k}\to[0,1]
supported near
\overline{U_{k}^{\prime}}
such that
\rho_{k}=1
over
U_{k}^{\prime}
. Also regard
\rho_{k}
as a function on
{\mathcal{U}}
. Then
\rho_{k}S_{k}^{\prime}
is a normally complex section of
{\mathcal{E}}
. Define

{\mathcal{S}}_{k}=(1-\rho_{k}){\mathcal{S}}_{k-1}+\rho_{k}S_{k}^{\prime}.

By construction, the section agrees with
{\mathcal{S}}_{k-1}
near
{\mathcal{Y}}_{k}
. Moreover, by construction, it is FOP transverse near
\psi_{i}(\overline{U_{k}^{\prime}})
. Lastly,

\|{\mathcal{S}}_{k}-{\mathcal{S}}_{0}\|_{C^{0}}\leq\|{\mathcal{S}}_{k}-{\mathcal{S}}_{k-1}\|_{C^{0}}+\|{\mathcal{S}}_{k-1}-{\mathcal{S}}_{0}\|_{C^{0}}\leq\left(1-\frac{1}{2^{k}}\right)\delta.

This proves the
k
-th case of the induction.

Finally, as the countable cover is locally finite, on any compact subset, the sequence
{\mathcal{S}}_{k}
stabilizes for
k
sufficiently large. Hence in the limit one obtains an FOP transverse section
{\mathcal{S}}
satisfying the required conditions.
∎

4.3.3.
Extension property II

We consider the problem of extending FOP transverse sections from a suborbifold
{\mathcal{X}}\subseteq{\mathcal{U}}
with an ordinary normal bundle (Definition
2.4
). Denote
{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{X}}:={\mathcal{E}}|_{\mathcal{X}}
. By the discussion of Subsection
2.3
,
({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{X}})
is an NC pair. Let
{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathcal{X}}:{\mathcal{X}}\to{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{X}}
be an FOP transverse section. Then by definition, there exists a straightening
({\mathcal{X}}^{\#},{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\#})
of
({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{X}})
such that
{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathcal{X}}
is an FOP transverse section with respect to this straightening. Then by Proposition
2.41
, there exists a straightening
({\mathcal{U}}^{\#},{\mathcal{E}}^{\#})
which extends
({\mathcal{X}}^{\#},{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\#})
. Moreover, the involved structures are all straightened along
N{\mathcal{X}}
. Let
\epsilon:{\mathcal{X}}\to{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
be a sufficiently small smooth function with an associated disk bundle
N^{\epsilon}{\mathcal{X}}
. Then the normal exponential map
\exp^{N{\mathcal{X}}}:N^{\epsilon}{\mathcal{X}}\to{\mathcal{U}}
is an open embedding, covered by a bundle map

{\rm par}^{N{\mathcal{X}}}:\pi_{N{\mathcal{X}}}^{*}{\mathcal{E}}|_{\mathcal{X}}\to{\mathcal{E}}

which defines a bundle isomorphism when restricting
{\mathcal{E}}
along the image of
\exp^{N{\mathcal{X}}}
. We claim that the pullback

{\rm par}^{N{\mathcal{X}}}\circ(\pi_{N{\mathcal{X}}}^{*}{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathcal{X}})\circ(\exp^{N{\mathcal{X}}})^{-1}:|N^{\epsilon}{\mathcal{X}}|\to{\mathcal{E}}

(4.2)

is FOP transverse. It suffices to consider this claim locally. Let
\hat{C}=(G,U,E,\hat{\psi})
be a bundle chart centered at a point
x\in{\mathcal{X}}
. Then by definition,
\psi^{-1}({\mathcal{X}})
is a
G
-invariant submanifold
X\subseteq U
with a normal bundle
NX\to X
. The section
{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathcal{X}}
is pulled back to an NC section

S_{X}:X\to E|_{X}

which induces an NC bundle map (for some
\delta:X_{G_{x}}\to{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
)

S_{X,G_{x}}^{\#}:N^{\delta}X_{G_{x}}\to E|_{X_{G_{x}}}.

As
G_{x}
acts trivialy on the normal bundle
NX\to X
, the trivial factor in the normal direction in
NX
does not affects the FOP transversality. Hence the pullback

(\pi^{NX})^{*}S_{X,G_{x}}^{\#}

is still FOP transverse. Therefore, after extending the FOP transverse section (
4.2
) using a smooth cut-off function, we can apply Proposition
4.12
to get the desired extension.

4.3.4.
Product property

For
i=1,2
, let
({\mathcal{U}}_{i},{\mathcal{E}}_{i})
be an NC pair. Given
{\mathcal{S}}_{i}\in\Gamma^{\rm FOP}({\mathcal{U}}_{i},{\mathcal{E}}_{i})
. Denote
{\mathcal{U}}={\mathcal{U}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{U}}_{2}
,
{\mathcal{E}}={\mathcal{E}}_{1}\boxplus{\mathcal{E}}_{2}
, and
{\mathcal{S}}={\mathcal{S}}_{1}\boxplus{\mathcal{S}}_{2}
. We prove that
{\mathcal{S}}
is FOP transverse. Indeed, by definition, there exists a straightening
({\mathcal{U}}_{i}^{\#},{\mathcal{E}}_{i}^{\#})
on
({\mathcal{U}}_{i},{\mathcal{E}}_{i})
such that

{\mathcal{S}}_{i}\in\Gamma^{\rm FOP}({\mathcal{U}}_{i}^{\#},{\mathcal{E}}_{i}^{\#}).

The two straightenings induce a straightening on
({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}})
by taking the product. We check that
{\mathcal{S}}
is FOP transverse at each point
p=(p_{1},p_{2})
with respect to the product straightening. If
\hat{C}_{i}=(G_{i},U_{i},E_{i},\hat{\psi}_{i})
is a bundle chart of
{\mathcal{E}}_{i}
centered at
p_{i}
, then

\hat{C}=(G,U,E,\hat{\psi}):=\hat{C}_{1}\times\hat{C}_{2}:=(G_{1}\times G_{2},U_{1}\times U_{2},E_{1}\boxplus E_{2},\hat{\psi}_{1}\boxplus\hat{\psi}_{2})

is a bundle chart of
{\mathcal{E}}
. Let

S_{G_{i}}^{\#}:N^{\epsilon_{i}}U_{i,G_{i}}\to E_{i}|_{U_{G_{i}}}

be the NC bundle maps induced from the straightenings. Then for
\epsilon<\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}
, the bundle map induced from the product straightening

S_{G}^{\#}=(S_{G_{1}}^{\#},S_{G_{2}}^{\#}):N^{\epsilon}U_{G}\to E|_{U_{G}}

is well-defined, and it is obviously an NC bundle map. Abbreviate
X_{i}=U_{i,G_{i}}
,
F_{i}=NU_{i,G_{i}}
,
E_{i}=E_{i}|_{U_{G_{i}}}
. Let
{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}:F_{i}\to\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{G_{i}}^{d}(F_{i},E_{i})
be a lift of
S_{G_{i}}^{\#}
. Then

{\mathfrak{p}}:=({\mathfrak{p}}_{1},{\mathfrak{p}}_{2}):F_{1}\boxplus F_{2}\to\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(F_{1}\boxplus F_{2},E_{1}\boxplus E_{2})

is a lift of
S_{G}^{\#}
. Then by Proposition
3.16
, the FOP transversality of
{\mathfrak{p}}_{1}
and
{\mathfrak{p}}_{2}
implies the FOP transversality of
{\mathfrak{p}}
. Hence
{\mathcal{S}}
is FOP transverse.

4.3.5.
Stratified transversality

Suppose
{\mathcal{S}}\in\Gamma^{\rm FOP}({\mathcal{U}}^{\#},{\mathcal{E}}^{\#})\subseteq\Gamma^{\rm FOP}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}})
. Let
\gamma
be represented by an NC triple
(G,V,W)
. Let
p\in{\mathcal{U}}_{\gamma}
. Then it suffices to consider on a chart containing
p
. Let
\hat{C}=(G,U,E,\hat{\psi})
be a bundle chart centered at
p
. Then
\psi:U_{G}\to{\mathcal{U}}_{\gamma}
gives a manifold chart of
{\mathcal{U}}_{\gamma}
. Let
S:U\to E
be the section corresponding to
{\mathcal{S}}
. Using the straightening one obtains the bundle map

S_{G}^{\#}:N^{\epsilon}U_{G}\to E|_{U_{G}}.

Using the basic decomposition
E|_{U_{G}}=E_{G}\oplus\check{E}_{G}
, one obtains the two components
S_{G}
and
\check{S}_{G}
of
S_{G}^{\#}
. Then by the definition of FOP transversality, the section

S_{G}:N^{\epsilon}U_{G}\to E_{G}

is transverse at
\psi^{-1}(p)\in U_{G}
. As the derivative of
S_{G}
in the normal direction to
U_{G}
vanishes, this is equivalent to that
S_{G}|_{U_{G}}
is transverse at
0
. This implies the stratified transversality.

4.3.6.
Stabilization property

Start with
{\mathcal{S}}\in\Gamma^{\rm FOP}({\mathcal{U}}^{\#},{\mathcal{E}}^{\#})\subseteq\Gamma^{\rm FOP}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}})
. Let
{\mathcal{F}}\to{\mathcal{U}}
be an NC vector bundle. Then by Proposition
2.34
, there exist a connection
\nabla^{\mathcal{F}}
straightened with respect to
g^{T{\mathcal{U}}}
, an inner product
h^{\mathcal{F}}
preserved by
\nabla^{\mathcal{F}}
, and a nearby NC structure
{\mathcal{I}}^{\mathcal{F}}
which is straightened with respect to
g^{T{\mathcal{U}}}
and
\nabla^{\mathcal{F}}
such that
(\nabla^{\mathcal{F}},h^{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{I}}^{\mathcal{F}})
is normally Hermitian. By Proposition
2.39
, the bundle metric
g^{T{\mathcal{F}}}
and the bundle NC structure
{\mathcal{I}}^{T{\mathcal{F}}}
induce a straightening
{\mathcal{F}}^{\#}
of
{\mathcal{F}}
. There are also straightenings

(\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{*}{\mathcal{E}})^{\#}\ {\rm and}\ (\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{*}{\mathcal{F}})^{\#}.

We claim

\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{*}{\mathcal{S}}\oplus\tau_{\mathcal{F}}\in\Gamma^{\rm FOP}\Big({\mathcal{F}}^{\#},(\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{*}{\mathcal{E}})^{\#}\oplus(\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{*}{\mathcal{F}})^{\#}\Big).

Still, it suffices to consider this problem locally on a single chart. Let
\hat{C}=(G,U,E,\hat{\psi})
be a bundle chart of
{\mathcal{E}}
. Assume
{\mathcal{F}}
is pulled back to a
G
-equivariant bundle
F\to U
on this chart, which can be achieved by shrinking
U
if necessary. Then
{\mathcal{S}}
is pulled back to an FOP transverse NC section
S:U\to E
. Consider the section

\tilde{S}:=\pi_{F}^{*}S\oplus\tau_{F}:F\to\pi_{F}^{*}E\oplus\pi_{F}^{*}F.

The bundle straightening construction implies that
\tilde{S}
is an NC section. Moreover, if
{\mathfrak{p}}:N^{\epsilon}U_{G}\to\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(NU_{G},E|_{U_{G}})
is a lift of
S_{G}^{\#}
, then there is a natural lift

\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}:NF_{G}\to\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(NF_{G},\pi_{F_{G}}^{*}E\oplus\pi_{F_{G}}^{*}F)\\
=\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(NF_{G},\pi_{F_{G}}^{*}E\oplus\pi_{F_{G}}^{*}F_{G}\oplus\pi_{F_{G}}^{*}\check{F}_{G})\\
=\pi_{F_{G}}^{*}\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(NU_{G},E|_{U_{G}})\oplus\pi_{F_{G}}^{*}\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(\check{F}_{G},\check{F}_{G})\oplus\pi_{F_{G}}^{*}F_{G}

of
\tilde{S}_{G}^{\#}
defined as follows. Use
u
to denote fiber coordinates of
F
; with respect to the basic decomposition
F=F_{G}\oplus\check{F}_{G}
, write
u=(u_{G},\check{u}_{G})
. Then

\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}(x,v,u_{G},\check{u}_{G})=\Big({\mathfrak{p}}(x,v),{\rm Id}_{\check{F}_{G}},u_{G}\Big).

In terms of graphs, it follows that

\eta^{-1}({\rm graph}(\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}))={\rm graph}({\mathfrak{p}})

where
\eta
is the (family version of the) map (
3.4
). Then by Proposition
3.17
,
{\mathfrak{p}}
is FOP transverse at
x\in U_{G}
if and only if
\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}
is FOP transverse at
(x,0)\in F
.

4.3.7.
Stratification of zero locus

Suppose the virtual dimension of
({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}})
is
k
. Let
{\mathcal{S}}:{\mathcal{U}}\to{\mathcal{E}}
be an FOP transverse section. For each universal stratum
\theta\in{\mathfrak{Z}}_{k}^{\rm univ}
, we define the subset

{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)_{\theta}\subseteq{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)

by identifying its intersection with each chart. Recall that one has the map
\rho_{k}:{\mathfrak{Z}}_{k}^{\rm univ}\to\underline{\smash{\mathbb{\Gamma}}}_{k}^{\rm NC}
. For a bundle chart
\hat{C}=(G,U,E,\hat{\psi})
, if
\psi(U)\cap{\mathcal{U}}_{\rho_{k}(\theta)}=\emptyset
, then
{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)_{\theta}\cap\psi(U)=\emptyset
. Otherwise, suppose
\rho_{k}(\theta)
is represented by a triple
(H,V,W)
where
H\subseteq_{U}G
,
V
is isomorphic to fibers of
TU|_{U_{H}}
and
W
is isomorphic to fibers of
E|_{U_{H}}
. Choose a lift

{\mathfrak{p}}:N^{\epsilon}U_{H}\to\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(NU_{H},E|_{U_{H}})

of the induced bundle map
S_{H}^{\#}:N^{\epsilon}U_{H}\to E|_{U_{H}}
. Then one has

(S_{H}^{\#})^{-1}(0)={\rm proj}_{1}\Big({\rm graph}({\mathfrak{p}})\cap Z^{d}(H,NU_{H},E|_{U_{H}})\Big)

where

{\rm proj}_{1}:M^{d}(H,NU_{H},E|_{U_{H}})=NU_{H}\oplus\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{H}^{d}(NU_{H},E|_{U_{H}})\to NU_{H}

is the projection onto the first factor.
Notice that
\theta
is represented by an element in
Z_{\theta}^{d}\in{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(H,V,W)
. Then using the local triviality of the canonical Whitney stratification, we can define

(S_{H}^{\#})^{-1}(0)_{\theta}:={\rm proj}_{1}\Big({\rm graph}({\mathfrak{p}})\cap Z^{d}_{\theta}\Big).

Then define
{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)_{\theta}
to be the union of images of all
(S_{H}^{\#})^{-1}(0)_{\theta}
under
\psi
. The FOP transversality condition implies that
{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)_{\theta}
is a smooth submanifold of
{\mathcal{U}}_{\rho_{k}(\theta)}
of dimension
k+n_{k}(\theta)
. Therefore we have constructed a decomposition

{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)=\bigsqcup_{\theta\in{\mathfrak{Z}}_{k}^{\rm univ}}{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)_{\theta}.

We need to prove that after we remove empty pieces, this partition makes
{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)
a Thom–Mather stratified space (Definition
C.1
). We first prove that it is a stratification. As locally this partition comes from a Whitney stratification on each orbifold chart, the partition is locally finite and each stratum is locally closed. It remains to verify the axiom of frontier (see Definition
2.11
), namely,

{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)_{\theta}\cap\overline{{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)_{\theta^{\prime}}}\neq\emptyset\Longrightarrow{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)_{\theta}\subseteq\overline{{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)_{\theta^{\prime}}}.

If
x\in{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)_{\theta}\cap\overline{{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)_{\theta^{\prime}}}
, choose a bundle chart
\hat{C}=(G,U,E,\hat{\psi})
centered at
x
. Let
V
be the fiber of
TU|_{U_{G}}
and
W
be the fiber of
E|_{U_{G}}
. Then the stable isotropy type
\rho_{k}(\theta)\in\underline{\smash{\mathbb{\Gamma}}}_{k}^{\rm NC}
is represented by
(G,V,W)
. Then for
d
sufficiently large,
\theta
corresponds to a
G
-fixed stratum
Z_{\alpha}\in{\mathfrak{Z}}^{d}(G,V,W)
and
\theta^{\prime}
corresponds to the
G
-orbit of some stratum
Z_{\alpha^{\prime}}\geq Z_{\alpha}
. Then if
y\in{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)_{\theta}
, choose a bundle chart centered at
y
and denoted, by abuse of notation, by
\hat{C}=(G,U,E,\hat{\psi})
. Let
S:U\to E
be the pullback of
{\mathcal{S}}
to
\hat{C}
. Then choose a lift of
S_{G}^{\#}

{\mathfrak{p}}:N^{\epsilon}U_{G}\to\widehat{\rm Poly}{}_{G}^{d}(NU_{G},E|_{U_{G}}).

Then

y\in\psi\Big({\rm proj}_{1}\Big({\rm graph}({\mathfrak{p}})\cap Z_{\alpha}^{d}(G,NU_{G},E|_{U_{G}})\Big)\Big).

As
Z_{\alpha}\leq Z_{\alpha^{\prime}}
(which means
Z_{\alpha}\subseteq\overline{Z_{\alpha^{\prime}}}
), by Lemma
B.2
and its proof, one sees

y\in\psi\Big({\rm proj}_{1}\Big(\overline{{\rm graph}({\mathfrak{p}})\cap Z_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{d}(G,NU_{G},E|_{U_{G}})}\Big)\Big).

It follows that
y\in\overline{{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)_{\theta^{\prime}}}
.

Lastly we need to show that the decomposition of
{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)
by the smooth manifolds
{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)_{\theta}
makes the zero locus a Thom–Mather stratified space. Indeed, locally the preimage of
{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)
in each chart is a Whitney stratified space, hence it is a Whitney stratified set in
{\mathcal{U}}
in the sense of Definition
C.16
. Then by Theorem
C.19
, Proposition
C.18
, and Definition
C.1
,
{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)
is a Thom–Mather stratified space.

5.
Integral Euler Cycles

In this section, we introduce aspects of the bordism theory of derived orbifolds and derive homological consequences from zero loci of FOP transverse sections. We will not discuss such bordism theories in full generality; instead, we only introduce concepts that are needed to state our results. The interested reader can refer to
[
Par22
]
and
[
Par23
]
for a comprehensive treatment.

5.1.
Derived orbifold bordism

Definition 5.1
.

A
derived orbifold
(with or without boundary) is a triple
{\mathcal{D}}=({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}})
where
{\mathcal{U}}
is an effective orbifold (with or without boundary),
{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{U}}
is an orbifold vector bundle, and
{\mathcal{S}}:{\mathcal{U}}\to{\mathcal{E}}
is a continuous section.

(1)

{\mathcal{D}}
is called
compact
if
{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)
is compact.

(2)

An
orientation
on
{\mathcal{D}}
is an orientation on the orbifold real vector bundle
T{\mathcal{U}}\otimes{\mathcal{E}}^{*}
.

(3)

A
normal complex structure
on
{\mathcal{D}}
consists of an NC structure on
{\mathcal{U}}
and an NC structure on
{\mathcal{E}}
. Two NC structures are concordant if the corresponding pairs of NC structures on
{\mathcal{U}}
and
{\mathcal{E}}
are concordant.

(4)

An
oriented and normally complex
(ONC for short) derived orbifold chart is a derived orbifold chart
{\mathcal{D}}
together with an orientation and an NC structure.

(5)

The
virtual dimension
of
{\mathcal{D}}
is the integer

{\rm dim}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm vir}({\mathcal{D}})={\rm dim}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{U}})-{\rm rank}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{E}}).

We also consider a more special structure called
stable complex structure
.

Definition 5.2
.

Let
{\mathcal{D}}=({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}})
be a derived orbifold.

(1)

A
stable complex strucutre
on
{\mathcal{E}}
is an equivalence class of quadruples

(k,{\mathcal{F}}_{0},{\mathcal{F}}_{1},\phi)

where
k\geq 0
is an integer,
{\mathcal{F}}_{0},{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{U}}
are complex vector bundles, and

\phi:\underline{\smash{\mathbb{R}}}^{\oplus k}\oplus{\mathcal{E}}\oplus{\mathcal{F}}_{0}\cong{\mathcal{F}}_{1}

is an isomorphism of orbifold vector bundles. Here
\underline{\smash{\mathbb{R}}}^{\oplus k}
denotes the trivial rank
k
bundle. The equivalence relation
\sim
is generated by the following two relations: 1) we require that

(k,{\mathcal{F}}_{0},{\mathcal{F}}_{1},\phi)\sim(k+2,{\mathcal{F}}_{0},\underline{\smash{\mathbb{C}}}\oplus{\mathcal{F}}_{1},\phi_{0}\oplus\phi)

where

\phi_{0}:\underline{\smash{\mathbb{R}}}^{\oplus 2}\to\underline{\smash{\mathbb{C}}}
is the map
(x,y)\mapsto x+{\bf i}y
; 2) we require that

(k,{\mathcal{F}}_{0},{\mathcal{F}}_{1},\phi)\sim(k,{\mathcal{F}}_{0}\oplus{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\oplus{\mathcal{F}},\phi\oplus{\rm Id}_{\mathcal{F}})

where
{\mathcal{F}}\to{\mathcal{U}}
is an arbitrary complex vector bundle.

(2)

A
stable complex structure
on
{\mathcal{D}}
consists of a stable complex structure on
T{\mathcal{U}}
and a stable complex structure on
{\mathcal{E}}
.

There are a few standard and basic operations on derived orbifolds. For a derived orbifold with boundary
{\mathcal{D}}=({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}})
, one can restrict to the boundary

\partial{\mathcal{D}}=(\partial{\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}}|_{\partial{\mathcal{U}}},{\mathcal{S}}|_{\partial{\mathcal{U}}}).

If
{\mathcal{D}}
is ONC, so is
\partial{\mathcal{D}}
. For derived orbifolds without boundary, there are the operation
{\mathcal{D}}\mapsto-{\mathcal{D}}
by reversing the orientation and the operation of taking disjoint union.

Now we consider the derived bordism group of topological spaces. We refrain from using the language of generalized homology theories. The introduction of the bordism group is merely for the convenience of stating the results.

Definition 5.3
.

Let
X
be a topological space. The
oriented and normally complex derived orbifold bordism group
resp.
stably complex derived orbifold group
of
X
, denoted by
{\rm d}\Omega_{*}^{\rm ONC}(X)
resp.
{\rm d}\Omega_{*}^{\rm U}(X)
, is the set of equivalence classes of quadruples
({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}},\kappa)
where
{\mathcal{D}}=({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}})
is a compact ONC resp. stably complex derived orbifold without boundary and
\kappa:{\mathcal{U}}\to X
is a continuous map, while the equivalence relation is generated by the following relations

(1)

(Shrinking)

({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}},\kappa)\sim({\mathcal{U}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime},\kappa^{\prime})
if
{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}\subseteq{\mathcal{U}}
is an open neighborhood of
{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)
,
{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}={\mathcal{E}}|_{{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}}
,
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}={\mathcal{S}}|_{{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}}
, and
\kappa^{\prime}=\kappa|_{{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}}
.

(2)

(Stabilization)

({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}},\kappa)\sim(\hat{\mathcal{U}},\hat{\mathcal{E}},\hat{\mathcal{S}},\hat{\kappa})
if there is a NC resp. complex vector bundle
\pi_{\mathcal{F}}:{\mathcal{F}}\to{\mathcal{U}}
such that
\hat{\mathcal{U}}={\mathcal{F}}
,
\hat{\mathcal{E}}=\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{*}{\mathcal{E}}\oplus\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{*}{\mathcal{F}}
,
\hat{\mathcal{S}}=\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{*}{\mathcal{S}}\oplus\tau_{\mathcal{F}}
, and
\hat{\kappa}:{\mathcal{F}}\to X
is any extension of the induced map along the zero section.

(3)

(Cobordism)

({\mathcal{U}}_{0},{\mathcal{E}}_{0},{\mathcal{S}}_{0},\kappa_{0})\sim({\mathcal{U}}_{1},{\mathcal{E}}_{1},{\mathcal{S}}_{1},\kappa_{1})
if there is a compact ONC resp. stably complex derived orbifold
\tilde{\mathcal{D}}=(\tilde{\mathcal{U}},\tilde{\mathcal{E}},\tilde{\mathcal{S}})
with
\partial\tilde{\mathcal{D}}\cong(-{\mathcal{D}}_{0})\sqcup{\mathcal{D}}_{1}
and a continuous map
\tilde{\kappa}:\tilde{\mathcal{U}}\to X
such that
\tilde{\kappa}|_{\partial\tilde{\mathcal{U}}}
coincides with
\kappa_{0}\sqcup\kappa_{1}
.

The bordism sets
{\rm d}\Omega_{*}^{\rm ONC}(X)
and
{\rm d}\Omega_{*}^{\rm U}(X)
are graded abelian groups with addition induced by disjoint union and grading defined by the virtual dimension. It is easy to see that
{\rm d}\Omega_{*}^{\rm ONC}
is a functor from topological spaces to graded abelian groups. Recall that there are the more classical bordism theories, the oriented bordism
\Omega_{*}^{\rm SO}
and stable complex bordism
\Omega_{*}^{\rm U}
.

Proposition 5.4
.

There is a natural transformation (the dotted arrow) making the following diagram commute (the other arrows are all naturally defined).

\textstyle{\Omega_{*}^{\rm U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm d}\Omega_{*}^{\rm U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Omega_{*}^{\rm SO}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm d}\Omega_{*}^{\rm ONC}}

Proof.

Since complex vector bundles are canonically oriented, a stable complex structure on a vector bundle naturally induces an orientation. To obtain an NC structure, one needs to make certain choices. Suppose
{\mathcal{D}}=({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}})
is a stably complex derived orbifold. By definition, there exist two quadruples
(k,{\mathcal{F}}_{0},{\mathcal{F}}_{1},\phi)
and
(l,{\mathcal{E}}_{0},{\mathcal{E}}_{1},\eta)
where

\displaystyle\ \phi:\underline{\smash{\mathbb{R}}}^{\oplus k}\oplus T{\mathcal{U}}\oplus{\mathcal{F}}_{0}\cong{\mathcal{F}}_{1},\

\displaystyle\ \eta:\underline{\smash{\mathbb{R}}}^{\oplus l}\oplus{\mathcal{E}}\oplus{\mathcal{E}}_{0}\cong{\mathcal{E}}_{1}

are isomorphisms. By taking stabilizations, which induce equivalent quadruples, we can assume
{\mathcal{F}}_{0}={\mathcal{E}}_{0}
. The above two isomorphisms can be pulled back to the total space of
{\mathcal{F}}_{0}
as

\pi_{{\mathcal{F}}_{0}}^{*}\phi:\underline{\smash{\mathbb{R}}}^{\oplus k}\oplus\pi_{{\mathcal{F}}_{0}}^{*}T{\mathcal{U}}\oplus\pi_{{\mathcal{F}}_{0}}^{*}{\mathcal{F}}_{0}\cong\pi_{{\mathcal{F}}_{0}}^{*}{\mathcal{F}}_{1}

and

\pi_{{\mathcal{F}}_{0}}^{*}\eta:\underline{\smash{\mathbb{R}}}^{\oplus l}\oplus\pi_{{\mathcal{F}}_{0}}^{*}{\mathcal{E}}\oplus\pi_{{\mathcal{F}}_{0}}^{*}{\mathcal{F}}_{0}\cong\pi_{{\mathcal{F}}_{0}}^{*}{\mathcal{E}}_{1}.

Upon choosing a connection on
{\mathcal{F}}_{0}
inducing a bundle isomorphism

\Delta:\pi_{{\mathcal{F}}_{0}}^{*}T{\mathcal{U}}\oplus\pi_{{\mathcal{F}}_{0}}^{*}{\mathcal{F}}_{0}\cong T{\mathcal{F}}_{0},

the pullback isomorphism
\pi_{{\mathcal{F}}_{0}}^{*}\phi
becomes an isomorphism

\underline{\smash{\mathbb{R}}}^{\oplus k}\oplus T{\mathcal{F}}_{0}\cong\pi_{{\mathcal{F}}_{0}}^{*}{\mathcal{F}}_{1}.

As
\underline{\smash{\mathbb{R}}}^{\oplus k}
and
\underline{\smash{\mathbb{R}}}^{\oplus l}
do not affect the normal direction, the above two isomorphism induce a normal complex structure on the stabilization of
{\mathcal{D}}
by
{\mathcal{F}}_{0}
. Note that the space of connections on
{\mathcal{F}}_{0}
is contractible; moreover, for different presentations of the stable complex structures on
{\mathcal{D}}
, Definition
5.2
implies that the induced normal complex structures agree by an extension of the connection. Therefore, although the specific construction requires choices, the resulting bordism class in
{\rm d}\Omega_{*}^{\rm ONC}
is canonically defined.
∎

5.2.
Definition of the Euler cycles

There is a natural transformation from the oriented bordism to the integral homology

\Omega_{*}^{\rm SO}(\cdot)\to H_{*}(\cdot;{\mathbb{Z}})

defined by pushing forward the fundamental classes of compact oriented manifolds. A virtual fundamental class can be viewed as a lift of this natural transformation to the derived orbifold bordism, possibly after changing the coefficients.

Theorem 5.5
.

For
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
and a universal stratum
\theta\in{\mathfrak{Z}}_{k}^{\rm univ}
, there is a natural transformation

{\rm FOP}_{\theta}:{\rm d}\Omega_{k}^{\rm ONC}(\cdot)\to H_{k+n_{k}(\theta)}(\cdot;{\mathbb{Z}})

such that for the special maximal
\theta_{k}\in{\mathfrak{Z}}_{k}^{\rm univ}
(corresponding to the isotropy free part of the zero locus), the composition

\Omega_{k}^{\rm SO}\to{\rm d}\Omega_{k}^{\rm ONC}\xrightarrow{{\rm FOP}_{\theta_{k}}}H_{k}(\cdot;{\mathbb{Z}})

agrees with the natural transformation
\Omega_{k}^{\rm SO}(\cdot)\to H_{k}(\cdot;{\mathbb{Z}})
which takes a closed oriented manifold to its fundamental class.

The proof is to construct the Euler cycles via FOP transverse perturbations.

Definition 5.6
.

Let
{\mathcal{D}}=({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}}_{0})
be a compact derived orbifold. A
perturbation
of
{\mathcal{D}}
is a section
{\mathcal{S}}:{\mathcal{U}}\to{\mathcal{E}}
satisfying the following condition: there exists a precompact open neighborhood
{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}
of
{\mathcal{S}}_{0}^{-1}(0)
and an inner product on
{\mathcal{E}}
such that

|{\mathcal{S}}_{0}(x)-{\mathcal{S}}(x)|<|{\mathcal{S}}_{0}(x)|\ \ \forall x\notin\overline{{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}}.

In particular,
{\mathcal{S}}
does not vanish outside
\overline{{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}}
.

Lemma 5.7
.

Let
{\mathcal{D}}=({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}}_{0})
be a compact and NC derived orbifold. Then there exists a smooth FOP transverse perturbation
{\mathcal{S}}:{\mathcal{U}}\to{\mathcal{E}}
. Moreover, if
{\mathcal{D}}
has boundary and
{\mathcal{S}}_{\partial}
is an FOP transverse perturbation of
\partial{\mathcal{D}}
, then one can take
{\mathcal{S}}
such that
{\mathcal{S}}|_{\partial{\mathcal{U}}}={\mathcal{S}}_{\partial}
.

Proof.

We can choose a precompact open neighborhood
{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}
of
{\mathcal{S}}_{0}^{-1}(0)
and an inner product on
{\mathcal{E}}
such that
|{\mathcal{S}}_{0}(x)|\geq 2\delta
for some
\delta>0
and all
x\notin{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}
. Then by the
C^{0}
-density of the space of FOP transverse sections, there exists an FOP transverse
{\mathcal{S}}:{\mathcal{U}}\to{\mathcal{E}}
such that
\|{\mathcal{S}}-{\mathcal{S}}_{0}\|_{C^{0}}\leq\delta
, which defines a perturbation of
{\mathcal{D}}
. If
{\mathcal{S}}_{\partial}
is given, notice that
\partial{\mathcal{U}}\subseteq{\mathcal{U}}
can be viewed as a suborbifold with ordinary normal bundle (the collar). Then by (
Extension Property II
) of Theorem
1.1
, there exists an FOP perturbation extending
{\mathcal{S}}_{\partial}
to the interior.
∎

Proposition 5.8
.

Let
{\mathcal{D}}=({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}}_{0})
be a compact NC derived orbifold.

(1)

For each FOP transverse perturbation
{\mathcal{S}}
and each universal stratum
\theta
, the closure
\overline{{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)_{\theta}}
is a compact oriented Thom–Mather stratified pseudomanifold (Definition
C.6
).

(2)

If
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}
is an FOP transverse perturbation on the product
\tilde{\mathcal{D}}=[1,2]\times{\mathcal{D}}
with boundary restrictions denoted by
{\mathcal{S}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{S}}_{2}
respectively, then the closure
\overline{\tilde{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)_{\theta}}
is a cobordism of oriented Thom–Mather stratified pseudomanifolds (Definition
C.8
).

Proof.

For (1), by Theorem
1.1
, the closure
\overline{{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)_{\theta}}
is a compact oriented Thom–Mather stratified space; furthermore, based on the discussion in Section
4.3.7
, the frontier of
{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)_{\theta}
is the union of smooth manifolds of codimension two or higher as the same assertion holds for the universal zero locus. Hence
{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)_{\theta}
satisfies conditions for pseudomanifolds. For the same reason, in (2) the closure
\overline{\tilde{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)_{\theta}}
provides a cobordism.
∎

Proof of Theorem
5.5
.

Given an element
a\in{\rm d}\Omega_{*}^{\rm ONC}(X)
represented by
({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}}_{0},\kappa)
. Consider an FOP transverse perturbation
{\mathcal{S}}
. By (2) of Proposition
5.8
, the Thom–Mather stratified pseudomanifold
\overline{{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)_{\theta}}
carries a fundamental class

[\overline{{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)_{\theta}}]\in H_{n_{k}(\theta)}(\overline{{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)_{\theta}};{\mathbb{Z}}),

see Appendix
C
. Via the inclusion into
{\mathcal{U}}
and the map
\kappa:{\mathcal{U}}\to X
, we define

\chi_{\theta}^{\rm FOP}(a):=\kappa_{*}[\overline{{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)_{\theta}}]\in H_{n_{k}(\theta)}(X;{\mathbb{Z}}).

We only need to prove that this element only depends on the element in
{\rm d}\Omega_{*}^{\rm ONC}(X)
.

We first show that it is independent of the choice of FOP transverse perturbations. Indeed, if
{\mathcal{S}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{S}}_{2}
are two such perturbations, then (3) of Proposition
5.8
and Lemma
C.9
imply that the corresponding cycles are equal.

We then need to show that the class
\chi_{\theta}^{\rm FOP}(a)
is invariant under the equivalence relation defining
{\rm d}\Omega_{k}^{\rm ONC}(X)
. Indeed, the subsequent Lemma
5.9
, Lemma
5.10
, and Lemma
5.11
show that the generators of the equivalence relation, the pushforward class in
X
is invariant.

Lemma 5.9
.

Let
{\mathcal{D}}=({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}}_{0})
be a compact ONC derived orbifold without boundary and
\iota:{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{U}}
be an open embedding onto a neighborhood of
{\mathcal{S}}_{0}^{-1}(0)
and
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}
be the corresponding shrinking of
{\mathcal{D}}
. Then for any
\theta\in\Theta
,

\chi_{\theta}^{\rm FOP}({\mathcal{D}})=\iota_{*}(\chi_{\theta}^{\rm FOP}({\mathcal{D}}^{\prime})).

Proof.

One can first choose an FOP transverse perturbation
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
of
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}
. Then by using a cut-off function supported in
{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}
, one can find an FOP transverse perturbation
{\mathcal{S}}
of
{\mathcal{D}}
such that
\iota^{*}{\mathcal{S}}
coincides with
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
on a smaller neighborhood of
{\mathcal{S}}_{0}^{-1}(0)
such that
{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)=\iota(({\mathcal{S}}^{\prime})^{-1}(0))
. Then the lemma follows.
∎

Lemma 5.10
.

Let
{\mathcal{D}}=({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}}_{0})
be a compact ONC derived orbifold without boundary. Let
\hat{\mathcal{D}}
be the stabilization of
{\mathcal{D}}
by an NC vector bundle
{\mathcal{F}}\to{\mathcal{U}}
and let
\iota:{\mathcal{U}}\to{\mathcal{F}}
be the zero section. Then for each universal stratum
\theta\in\Theta
,

\chi_{\theta}^{\rm FOP}(\hat{\mathcal{D}})=\iota_{*}\Big(\chi_{\theta}^{\rm FOP}({\mathcal{D}})\Big).

(5.1)

Proof.

Choose an FOP transverse perturbation
{\mathcal{S}}
of
{\mathcal{D}}
. By the
(Stabilization Property)
of Theorem
1.1
, the stabilization
\hat{\mathcal{S}}
is FOP transverse; it is easy to check it is also a perturbation of
\hat{\mathcal{D}}
. Then

\iota({\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)_{\theta})=\hat{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)_{\theta}.

This is indeed an isomorphism of Thom–Mather stratified spaces and preserves orientation. Hence (
5.1
) holds.
∎

Lemma 5.11
.

Let
{\mathcal{D}}_{i}=({\mathcal{U}}_{i},{\mathcal{E}}_{i},{\mathcal{S}}_{0,i})
,
i=1,2
, be compact ONC derived orbifolds without boundary. Let
\tilde{\mathcal{D}}
be a compact ONC cobordism from
{\mathcal{D}}_{1}
to
{\mathcal{D}}_{2}
. Let
\iota:{\mathcal{U}}_{1}\sqcup{\mathcal{U}}_{2}\to\partial{\mathcal{U}}
be the diffeomorphis contained in the cobordism. Then for each universal stratum
\theta\in{\mathfrak{Z}}^{\rm univ}
, there exists a class
a_{\theta}\in H_{*}(\tilde{\mathcal{U}},\partial\tilde{\mathcal{U}};{\mathbb{Z}})
such that

\iota_{*}\left(\chi_{\theta}^{\rm FOP}({\mathcal{D}}_{2})-\chi_{\theta}^{\rm FOP}({\mathcal{D}}_{1})\right)=\partial a_{\theta}.

Proof.

Choose FOP transverse perturbations
{\mathcal{S}}_{i}:{\mathcal{U}}_{i}\to{\mathcal{E}}_{i}
of
{\mathcal{D}}_{i}
. Then by Lemma
5.7
, one can find an extension
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}:\tilde{\mathcal{U}}\to\tilde{\mathcal{E}}
as a perturbation of
\tilde{\mathcal{D}}
whose boundary restriction is
{\mathcal{S}}_{0,i}
respectively. Consider the stratum
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)_{\theta}\subseteq\tilde{\mathcal{U}}
which is an oriented manifold with boundary identified with
(-{\mathcal{S}}_{1}^{-1}(0)_{\theta})\sqcup({\mathcal{S}}_{2}^{-1}(0)_{\theta})
